+    assert.deepEqual(io.files(), before, 'a garbled --dry-run must never fall through to a real init');
+    assert.match(io.stderrText(), /#/, 'the error names the stray token');
+  });
+
+  it('--check with a stray trailing token exits 2 (read-only, no guessing)', async () => {
+    const io = seedIo();
+    const before = io.files();
+    const code = await cmdInit(['--check', 'foo'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 2);
+    assert.deepEqual(io.files(), before, 'a garbled --check must stay read-only');
+  });
 });

+import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
+import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
+import { makeIo } from '../helpers/fakeio.mjs';
+import { cmdStatus } from '../../core/src/commands/status.mjs';
+
+// Audit finding 11: the /baton:handoff ownership check reads `status --json`,
+// but the envelope carried only {bundleId, status, generation, updatedAt} —
+// no origin, so "is this bundle owned by another platform?" was unanswerable
+// from the documented command. status now surfaces origin + goal.
+
+const BUNDLE = {
+  schema: 'baton/bundle@1',
+  bundleId: 'b_status00000000',
+  generation: 3,
+  createdAt: '2026-07-13T00:00:00.000Z',
+  updatedAt: '2026-07-13T00:00:00.000Z',
+  origin: { platform: 'codex', model: 'gpt-5.6-sol', sessionHint: 'codex-s-9', unstable: false },
+  task: { goal: 'Ship the audit fold', constraints: [], acceptance: [] },
+  plan: { steps: [] },
+  decisions: [],
+  files: { touched: [] },
+  roles: { assignments: {} },
+  git: null,
+  handoff: { status: 'open', reason: null, reasonClass: null, toPlatformHint: null, finalizedAt: null, receive_log: [] },
+  journalSeq: 0,
+  compaction: { droppedDecisions: 0, note: null },
+  dedupeRing: [],
+};
+
+describe('status — surfaces bundle ownership', () => {
+  it('--json data carries origin {platform, sessionHint, unstable} and the goal', async () => {
+    const io = makeIo({ files: { '/repo/.handoff/bundle.json': JSON.stringify(BUNDLE, null, 2) + '\n' } });
+    assert.equal(await cmdStatus(['--json'], io), 0);
+    const { data } = JSON.parse(io.stdoutText());
+    assert.deepEqual(data.origin, { platform: 'codex', sessionHint: 'codex-s-9', unstable: false });
+    assert.equal(data.goal, 'Ship the audit fold');
+  });
+
+  it('bare mode names the owner too', async () => {
+    const io = makeIo({ files: { '/repo/.handoff/bundle.json': JSON.stringify(BUNDLE, null, 2) + '\n' } });
+    assert.equal(await cmdStatus([], io), 0);
+    assert.match(io.stdoutText(), /owner codex\/codex-s-9/);
+  });
+});

+import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
+import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
+import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import { join, dirname } from 'node:path';
+import { makeIo } from '../helpers/fakeio.mjs';
+import { prepare, commit } from '../../core/src/receive/txn.mjs';
+import { loadBundle } from '../../core/src/bundle/store.mjs';
+
+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+// Surface-audit fold (post-Gate-2): the receive token contract as a MODEL
+// actually drives it. Findings addressed here:
+//  - stale-token errors name WHICH bound input drifted (the generic six-way
+//    guess left models in a deterministic re-prepare loop);
+//  - a spent token re-committed by the SAME receiver is idempotent success
+//    (alreadyCommitted), never a re-prepare instruction that produces a
+//    duplicate receive (generation bumped twice, bogus degraded archive);
+//  - intake defaults from the sealed bundle: prepare/commit without --origin/
+//    --reason render and record the SEALED origin/reason instead of
+//    "unknown"/"unspecified".
+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+const here = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const repoRoot = join(here, '..', '..');
+const SIG_PATH = join(repoRoot, 'core', 'data', 'signatures.v1.json');
+const SIG_CONTENT = readFileSync(SIG_PATH, 'utf8');
+
+const ROOT = '/repo';
+const NOW = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z';
+const CONFIG_TEXT = JSON.stringify({
+  schema: 'baton/config@1',
+  roles: { planner: ['claude-code/claude-fable-5'], implementer: ['claude-code/claude-fable-5', 'codex/gpt-5.6-sol@xhigh'] },
+  platforms: { 'claude-code': {}, codex: {}, cursor: {} },
+  defaults: { 'claude-code': 'claude-fable-5', codex: 'gpt-5.6-sol', cursor: 'composer' },
+});
+const G1 = { branch: 'main', headSha: 'abc123', dirty: false, dirtySummary: [], contentDigest: 'gd-1' };
+const snapText = (b) => JSON.stringify(b, null, 2) + '\n';
+
+function sealedBundle(overrides = {}) {
+  return {
+    schema: 'baton/bundle@1',
+    bundleId: 'b_audit000000000',
+    generation: 1,
+    createdAt: NOW,
+    updatedAt: NOW,
+    origin: { platform: 'claude-code', model: 'claude-fable-5', sessionHint: 'sess-A', unstable: false },
+    task: { goal: 'Fold the audit', constraints: [], acceptance: [] },
+    plan: { steps: [{ id: 's1', title: 'Fix receive', status: 'active', note: null }] },
+    decisions: [],
+    files: { touched: [] },
+    roles: { assignments: {} },
+    git: { branch: 'main', headSha: 'abc123', dirty: false, dirtySummary: [], contentDigest: 'd0', summaryTruncated: false },
+    handoff: { status: 'sealed', reason: 'weekly Opus limit hit mid-task', reasonClass: 'usage-limit', toPlatformHint: 'codex', finalizedAt: NOW, receive_log: [] },
+    journalSeq: 5,
+    compaction: { droppedDecisions: 0, note: null },
+    dedupeRing: [],
+    ...overrides,
+  };
+}
+
+function txnIo(bundle) {
+  return makeIo({
+    files: {
+      [SIG_PATH]: SIG_CONTENT,
+      [`${ROOT}/baton.config.json`]: CONFIG_TEXT,
+      [`${ROOT}/.handoff/bundle.json`]: snapText(bundle),
+      [`${ROOT}/.handoff/journal.ndjson`]: '',
+    },
+    now: NOW,
+  });
+}
+const opts = (overrides = {}) => ({ platform: 'codex', origin: 'claude-code', reason: 'weekly Opus limit hit mid-task', gitSnapshot: G1, probes: null, sessionHint: 'codex-sess-1', ...overrides });
+
+// ===========================================================================
+describe('stale-token rejection names the drifted input', () => {
+  it('an intake-reason drift is named in the error, and re-prepare is still the remedy', () => {
+    const io = txnIo(sealedBundle());
+    const { token } = prepare(ROOT, opts({ reason: 'reason at prepare' }), io);
+    assert.throws(
+      () => commit(ROOT, token, opts({ reason: 'DIFFERENT reason at commit' }), io),
+      (/** @type {any} */ e) => /re-?prepare/i.test(e.message) && /intake reason\b/.test(e.message),
+      'the error names the drifted input (intake reason) so the model stops guessing',
+    );
+  });
+
+  it('a target-platform drift is named too', () => {
+    const io = txnIo(sealedBundle());
+    const { token } = prepare(ROOT, opts({ platform: 'codex' }), io);
+    assert.throws(
+      () => commit(ROOT, token, opts({ platform: 'cursor', sessionHint: 'cursor-s' }), io),
+      (/** @type {any} */ e) => /target platform/.test(e.message) && /target session/.test(e.message),
+      'platform and session drifts are both named',
+    );
+  });
+});
+
+// ===========================================================================
+describe('spent-token re-commit is idempotent (audit finding: duplicate-receive corruption)', () => {
+  it('re-committing the SAME token returns alreadyCommitted with zero mutation', () => {
+    const io = txnIo(sealedBundle());
+    const { token } = prepare(ROOT, opts(), io);
+    const first = commit(ROOT, token, opts(), io);
+    assert.equal(first.generation, 2);
+
+    const before = io.files();
+    const second = commit(ROOT, token, opts(), io);
+    assert.equal(second.alreadyCommitted, true, 'the retry reports the receive already landed');
+    assert.equal(second.generation, 2, 'and names the generation it landed as');
+    assert.deepEqual(io.files(), before, 'the idempotent retry mutates nothing');
+    assert.equal(loadBundle(ROOT, io).bundle.generation, 2, 'no duplicate receive: generation advanced exactly once');
+  });
+
+  it("a COMPETITOR's stale token still throws (different receipt, not idempotent)", () => {
+    const io = txnIo(sealedBundle());
+    const winner = prepare(ROOT, opts({ platform: 'codex', sessionHint: 'codex-s' }), io);
+    const loser = prepare(ROOT, opts({ platform: 'cursor', sessionHint: 'cursor-s' }), io);
+    commit(ROOT, winner.token, opts({ platform: 'codex', sessionHint: 'codex-s' }), io);
+    assert.throws(
+      () => commit(ROOT, loser.token, opts({ platform: 'cursor', sessionHint: 'cursor-s' }), io),
+      /re-?prepare/i,
+      'the losing receiver must re-prepare against the new generation',
+    );
+  });
+});
+
+// ===========================================================================
+describe('intake defaults from the sealed bundle (no more unknown/unspecified)', () => {
+  it('prepare without intake flags renders the SEALED origin and reason in the prompt', () => {
+    const io = txnIo(sealedBundle());
+    const out = prepare(ROOT, opts({ origin: 'unknown', reason: 'unspecified' }), io);
+    assert.ok(out.prompt.includes('Handed off from claude-code'), `prompt uses the sealed origin — got: ${out.prompt.split('\n')[2]}`);
+    assert.ok(out.prompt.includes('weekly Opus limit hit mid-task'), 'prompt uses the sealed reason');
+  });
+
+  it('prepare→commit both defaulting stays token-consistent, and the receive_log records the defaulted intake', () => {
+    const io = txnIo(sealedBundle());
+    const { token } = prepare(ROOT, opts({ origin: 'unknown', reason: 'unspecified' }), io);
+    const res = commit(ROOT, token, opts({ origin: 'unknown', reason: 'unspecified' }), io);
+    assert.equal(res.generation, 2, 'defaulted intake commits cleanly');
+    const entry = loadBundle(ROOT, io).bundle.handoff.receive_log.at(-1);
+    assert.equal(entry.origin, 'claude-code', 'the audit trail records the real origin, not the sentinel');
+    assert.equal(entry.reason, 'weekly Opus limit hit mid-task');
+  });
+
+  it('explicit intake flags still win over the sealed values', () => {
+    const io = txnIo(sealedBundle());
+    const out = prepare(ROOT, opts({ origin: 'cursor', reason: 'user preference' }), io);
+    assert.ok(out.prompt.includes('Handed off from cursor'), 'explicit --origin wins');
+    assert.ok(out.prompt.includes('user preference'), 'explicit --reason wins');
+  });
+});

+import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
+import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
+import { makeIo } from '../helpers/fakeio.mjs';
+import { parseFlagsStrict, PLATFORMS } from '../../core/src/commands/shared.mjs';
+import { cmdCheckpoint } from '../../core/src/commands/checkpoint.mjs';
+import { cmdDoctor } from '../../core/src/commands/doctor.mjs';
+import { cmdRemap } from '../../core/src/commands/remap.mjs';
+
+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+// Surface-audit fold: the lenient hand-rolled parser silently ate stray tokens
+// (a boolean flag's "value") and unknown flags, flipping SAFETY-RELEVANT flags
+// off with exit 0 — checkpoint --strict, doctor --strict, remap --native-only,
+// receive --print-prompt all fail-dangerous. docs/design/core.md §tooling
+// already promised strict parsing. parseFlagsStrict(args, spec) enforces it:
+// unknown flag, stray positional, or a missing string-flag value each return
+// {error} for a usage-error exit 2. --platform/--to values are validated
+// against the platform enum (a typo'd platform used to seed persistent bogus
+// state that rejected the real platform's checkpoints as foreign).
+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+describe('parseFlagsStrict', () => {
+  const SPEC = { platform: 'string', strict: 'boolean' };
+
+  it('parses known flags: boolean by presence, string by next token', () => {
+    const r = parseFlagsStrict(['--platform', 'codex', '--strict'], SPEC);
+    assert.equal(r.error, undefined);
+    assert.equal(r.flags.platform, 'codex');
+    assert.equal(r.flags.strict, true);
+  });
+
+  it('a stray token after a boolean flag is a hard error, never a silent flag-loss', () => {
+    const r = parseFlagsStrict(['--strict', 'oops'], SPEC);
+    assert.ok(r.error && /oops/.test(r.error), `the error names the stray token — got: ${r.error}`);
+  });
+
+  it('an unknown flag is a hard error naming it (typos no longer silently lose intent)', () => {
+    const r = parseFlagsStrict(['--platfrom', 'codex'], SPEC);
+    assert.ok(r.error && /--platfrom/.test(r.error));
+  });
+
+  it('a string flag followed by another KNOWN flag is a missing-value error', () => {
+    const r = parseFlagsStrict(['--platform', '--strict'], SPEC);
+    assert.ok(r.error && /--platform/.test(r.error) && /value/i.test(r.error));
+  });
+
+  it('json and root are implied common flags for every command', () => {
+    const r = parseFlagsStrict(['--json', '--root', '/x'], {});
+    assert.equal(r.error, undefined);
+    assert.equal(r.flags.json, true);
+    assert.equal(r.flags.root, '/x');
+  });
+
+  it('an empty-string value is consumed as a value, not a stray token', () => {
+    const r = parseFlagsStrict(['--platform', ''], SPEC);
+    assert.equal(r.error, undefined);
+    assert.equal(r.flags.platform, '');
+  });
+});
+
+describe('fail-dangerous flag loss is dead (command level)', () => {
+  it('checkpoint --strict with a stray token exits 2, not a silent non-strict 0', async () => {
+    const io = makeIo({ stdin: 'not json' });
+    const code = await cmdCheckpoint(['--platform', 'claude-code', '--strict', 'stray'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 2, 'usage error, never a soft exit that CI reads as green');
+  });
+
+  it('doctor --strict with a stray token exits 2', async () => {
+    const io = makeIo();
+    const code = await cmdDoctor(['--strict', 'stray'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 2);
+  });
+
+  it('remap --native-only with a stray token exits 2, never a silent cross-platform remap', async () => {
+    const io = makeIo();
+    const code = await cmdRemap(['--to', 'codex', '--native-only', 'stray'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 2);
+  });
+});
+
+describe('--platform / --to enum validation', () => {
+  it('PLATFORMS is the supported enum', () => {
+    assert.deepEqual(PLATFORMS, ['claude-code', 'codex', 'cursor']);
+  });
+
+  it('a typo’d checkpoint --platform exits 2 instead of seeding bogus persistent state', async () => {
+    const io = makeIo({ stdin: '{}' });
+    const code = await cmdCheckpoint(['--platform', 'claud-code'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 2);
+    assert.match(io.stderrText(), /claude-code\|codex\|cursor/, 'the error lists the valid platforms');
+  });
+
+  it('a typo’d remap --to exits 2', async () => {
+    const io = makeIo();
+    const code = await cmdRemap(['--to', 'kodex'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 2);
+  });
+});

+import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
+import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
+import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import { join, dirname } from 'node:path';
+import { makeIo } from '../helpers/fakeio.mjs';
+import { cmdInit } from '../../core/src/commands/init.mjs';
+import { planHarnessInit } from '../../core/src/scaffold/harness.mjs';
+
+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+// PATH-independence of hook commands (post-Gate-2 field finding).
+//
+// A GUI-launched harness (Cursor / Codex desktop app) runs its hook commands
+// with a minimal PATH that omits nvm/volta shims, so a bare `baton …` command
+// resolves to "command not found" and the checkpoint silently no-ops — observed
+// live on lesson-gate: the Cursor `stop` hook never fired. The fix: `baton init`
+// embeds an absolute `"<node>" "<baton.mjs>"` invocation, which needs no PATH.
+//
+// These tests run the REAL packaged adapter templates through the writer so
+// template drift (e.g. a future bare-`baton` entry) reds here.
+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+const here = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const repoRoot = join(here, '..', '..');
+const CODEX_TPL = join(repoRoot, 'adapters', 'codex', 'hooks.json');
+const CURSOR_TPL = join(repoRoot, 'adapters', 'cursor', 'hooks.json');
+const BATON_ENTRY = join(repoRoot, 'core', 'bin', 'baton.mjs');
+const FAKE_NODE = '/opt/fake/nvm/v24/bin/node';
+const ROOT = '/repo';
+const TPL_DIR = join(repoRoot, 'templates');
+
+// Base-template seeds so full cmdInit runs succeed (mirrors init.test.mjs).
+const BASE_TEMPLATES = {
+  [join(TPL_DIR, 'baton.config.json.tpl')]: JSON.stringify({ schema: 'baton/config@1', roles: {}, platforms: {}, defaults: {} }),
+  [join(TPL_DIR, 'AGENTS.md.tpl')]: '## Handoff protocol\n\nCheckpoint after each subtask.\n',
+  [join(TPL_DIR, 'CLAUDE.md.tpl')]: '@AGENTS.md\n',
+};
+const GIT_CLEAN = {
+  'git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD': { stdout: 'main\n' },
+  'git rev-parse HEAD': { stdout: 'abc123\n' },
+  'git status --porcelain': { stdout: '' },
+  'git diff --cached': { stdout: '' },
+  'git diff': { stdout: '' },
+  'git ls-files --others --exclude-standard': { stdout: '' },
+};
+
+// Seed the REAL packaged template bytes into memfs at their module-resolved paths.
+const realTemplates = {
+  [CODEX_TPL]: readFileSync(CODEX_TPL, 'utf8'),
+  [CURSOR_TPL]: readFileSync(CURSOR_TPL, 'utf8'),
+};
+function io(extra = {}) {
+  return makeIo({ files: { ...realTemplates, ...extra }, env: { HOME: '/home/u' }, execPath: FAKE_NODE });
+}
+const hooksAction = (actions, plat) => actions.find((a) => a.path.endsWith(`/.${plat}/hooks.json`));
+
+/** Every command / commandWindows string in a parsed manifest. */
+function allCommands(manifest) {
+  /** @type {string[]} */
+  const out = [];
+  const walk = (n) => {
+    if (Array.isArray(n)) return n.forEach(walk);
+    if (n && typeof n === 'object') {
+      for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(n)) {
+        if ((k === 'command' || k === 'commandWindows') && typeof v === 'string') out.push(v);
+        else walk(v);
+      }
+    }
+  };
+  walk(manifest.hooks ?? {});
+  return out;
+}
+
+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+// The absolute-path hook manifests are MACHINE-SPECIFIC (they embed this
+// machine's node path), so init must gitignore them — committing one would
+// break every other machine and node upgrade.
+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+describe('init gitignores the machine-specific hook manifests', () => {
+  it('planHarnessInit(--codex --cursor) plans a .gitignore write covering both hooks.json paths', () => {
+    const actions = planHarnessInit(ROOT, { codex: true, cursor: true }, io());
+    const gi = actions.find((a) => a.path === `${ROOT}/.gitignore`);
+    assert.ok(gi && gi.op === 'write', 'a .gitignore action is planned');
+    assert.ok(gi.preview.includes('.codex/hooks.json'), 'covers .codex/hooks.json');
+    assert.ok(gi.preview.includes('.cursor/hooks.json'), 'covers .cursor/hooks.json');
+  });
+
+  it('with no harness flags, no .gitignore action is planned by the harness writer', () => {
+    const actions = planHarnessInit(ROOT, {}, io());
+    assert.equal(actions.find((a) => a.path === `${ROOT}/.gitignore`), undefined);
+  });
+
+  it('full `init --codex --cursor` on a FRESH tree lands .handoff/ AND both hooks.json lines (no clobber between the two gitignore writers)', async () => {
+    const fio = makeIo({ files: { ...realTemplates, ...BASE_TEMPLATES, [`${ROOT}/package.json`]: '{}' }, execResults: GIT_CLEAN, env: { HOME: '/home/u' }, execPath: FAKE_NODE });
+    const code = await cmdInit(['--codex', '--cursor'], fio);
+    assert.equal(code, 0, `init should succeed; stderr: ${fio.stderrText()}`);
+    const gi = fio.files()[`${ROOT}/.gitignore`];
+    assert.ok(gi, '.gitignore exists');
+    for (const line of ['.handoff/', '.codex/hooks.json', '.cursor/hooks.json']) {
+      assert.ok(gi.includes(line), `.gitignore covers ${line} — got:\n${gi}`);
+    }
+  });
+
+  it('a second full init is a byte-identical no-op (gitignore lines are not duplicated)', async () => {
+    const fio = makeIo({ files: { ...realTemplates, ...BASE_TEMPLATES, [`${ROOT}/package.json`]: '{}' }, execResults: GIT_CLEAN, env: { HOME: '/home/u' }, execPath: FAKE_NODE });
+    await cmdInit(['--codex', '--cursor'], fio);
+    const after = fio.files();
+    await cmdInit(['--codex', '--cursor'], fio);
+    assert.deepEqual(fio.files(), after, 'second run mutates nothing');
+  });
+});
+
+for (const [plat, flag] of [['codex', 'codex'], ['cursor', 'cursor']]) {
+  describe(`baton init --${flag} — hook commands are absolute (PATH-independent)`, () => {
+    it('every command embeds the injected node binary + absolute baton.mjs, never a bare `baton`', () => {
+      const a = hooksAction(planHarnessInit(ROOT, { [flag]: true }, io()), plat);
+      assert.ok(a && a.op === 'write', `a .${plat}/hooks.json write is planned`);
+      const cmds = allCommands(JSON.parse(a.preview));
+      assert.ok(cmds.length > 0, 'the manifest carries hook commands');
+      for (const c of cmds) {
+        assert.ok(c.includes(`"${FAKE_NODE}"`), `command embeds the absolute node binary — got: ${c}`);
+        assert.ok(c.includes(`"${BATON_ENTRY}"`), `command embeds the absolute baton.mjs script — got: ${c}`);
+        // No token is a bare `baton` (would depend on PATH). The only `baton`
+        // substring allowed is inside the quoted absolute baton.mjs path.
+        assert.ok(!/(^|\s)baton(\s|$)/.test(c), `no bare \`baton\` command word survives — got: ${c}`);
+      }
+    });
+
+    it('resolves as an executable pair: first token = node binary, second = baton.mjs', () => {
+      const a = hooksAction(planHarnessInit(ROOT, { [flag]: true }, io()), plat);
+      const cmds = allCommands(JSON.parse(a.preview)).filter((c) => !c.startsWith('cmd /c'));
+      const m = cmds[0].match(/^"([^"]+)"\s+"([^"]+)"/);
+      assert.ok(m, `command starts with two quoted absolute paths — got: ${cmds[0]}`);
+      assert.equal(m[1], FAKE_NODE, 'first token is the absolute node binary');
+      assert.ok(m[2].endsWith('/core/bin/baton.mjs'), 'second token is the absolute baton entry script');
+    });
+
+    it('re-init REPLACES a stale bare-`baton` entry — no duplicate, no lingering PATH-dependent command', () => {
+      const stale = plat === 'codex'
+        ? { hooks: { Stop: [{ hooks: [{ type: 'command', command: 'baton checkpoint --platform codex' }] }] } }
+        : { version: 1, hooks: { stop: [{ command: 'baton checkpoint --platform cursor' }] } };
+      const a = hooksAction(planHarnessInit(ROOT, { [flag]: true }, io({ [`${ROOT}/.${plat}/hooks.json`]: JSON.stringify(stale) })), plat);
+      assert.ok(a && a.op === 'write', 're-init over a stale bare entry plans a write (the replacement)');
+      const cmds = allCommands(JSON.parse(a.preview));
+      assert.ok(!cmds.some((c) => c === 'baton checkpoint --platform ' + plat), 'the stale bare-`baton` command is gone (replaced, not duplicated)');
+      assert.ok(cmds.every((c) => c.includes(`"${BATON_ENTRY}"`)), 'every surviving baton command is absolute');
+    });
+
+    it('preserves a user hook while rewriting baton’s own', () => {
+      const userEntry = plat === 'codex'
+        ? { hooks: [{ type: 'command', command: 'USER_KEEP_ME' }] }
+        : { command: 'USER_KEEP_ME' };
+      const ev = plat === 'codex' ? 'Stop' : 'stop';
+      const existing = plat === 'codex'
+        ? { hooks: { [ev]: [userEntry] } }
+        : { version: 1, hooks: { [ev]: [userEntry] } };
+      const a = hooksAction(planHarnessInit(ROOT, { [flag]: true }, io({ [`${ROOT}/.${plat}/hooks.json`]: JSON.stringify(existing) })), plat);
+      const merged = JSON.parse(a.preview);
+      assert.ok(JSON.stringify(merged.hooks[ev]).includes('USER_KEEP_ME'), 'the user entry survives the merge');
+      assert.ok(allCommands(merged).some((c) => c.includes(`"${BATON_ENTRY}"`)), 'baton’s own entry is present and absolute');
+    });
+
+    it('commandWindows survives cmd.exe /c quote-stripping (whole command wrapped in an outer quote pair)', () => {
+      // cmd /c strips the first and last quote when the command starts with a
+      // quote and contains more than one pair — `cmd /c "node" "script" args`
+      // therefore executes `node" "script...`. The documented remedy is an
+      // OUTER wrapping pair: cmd /c ""node" "script" args".
+      const a = hooksAction(planHarnessInit(ROOT, { [flag]: true }, io()), plat);
+      const winCmds = allCommands(JSON.parse(a.preview)).filter((c) => c.startsWith('cmd /c'));
+      for (const c of winCmds) {
+        assert.match(c, /^cmd \/c ""[^"]+" "[^"]+".*"$/, `the whole command is wrapped in an outer quote pair — got: ${c}`);
+      }
+      if (plat === 'codex') assert.ok(winCmds.length > 0, 'codex template carries commandWindows variants');
+    });
+
+    it('the checkpoint hook declares its event via --trigger so doctor’s canary can identify it', () => {
+      const canary = plat === 'codex' ? 'Stop' : 'stop';
+      const manifest = JSON.parse(hooksAction(planHarnessInit(ROOT, { [flag]: true }, io()), plat).preview);
+      const checkpointCmds = allCommands({ hooks: { [canary]: manifest.hooks[canary] } });
+      assert.ok(
+        checkpointCmds.some((c) => c.includes(`--trigger ${canary}`)),
+        `the ${canary} checkpoint command passes --trigger ${canary} — got: ${checkpointCmds.join(' | ')}`,
+      );
+    });
+
+    it('a second init is a byte-identical no-op (idempotent under the deterministic invocation)', () => {
+      const first = hooksAction(planHarnessInit(ROOT, { [flag]: true }, io()), plat);
+      const seeded = io({ [`${ROOT}/.${plat}/hooks.json`]: first.preview });
+      const second = hooksAction(planHarnessInit(ROOT, { [flag]: true }, seeded), plat);
+      assert.equal(second.op, 'skip', 'the second init changes nothing');
+    });
+  });
+}

+describe('checkpoint — tolerant stdin shapes (live /baton:handoff failure)', () => {
+  // Field finding: the handoff command says "send baton/event@1 events on
+  // stdin" and a live model sent one event PER LINE (NDJSON). The old parser
+  // rejected everything but a single JSON value, so the narrative checkpoint
+  // silently failed. Accept the shapes a model plausibly produces: a single
+  // {schema, events} object (canonical), a bare array of events, and NDJSON
+  // where every line is an event object — anything else stays bad-stdin.
+  it('NDJSON stdin (one event per line) journals every line', async () => {
+    const io = makeIo({
+      files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(mkBundle()) },
+      stdin:
+        JSON.stringify({ schema: 'baton/event@1', type: 'decision', payload: { summary: 'ndjson-line-1' } }) +
+        '\n' +
+        JSON.stringify({ type: 'decision', payload: { summary: 'ndjson-line-2' } }) +
+        '\n',
+    });
+    const code = await cmdCheckpoint(['--platform', 'claude-code'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 0);
+    const sums = journalEntries(io).map((e) => e.payload?.summary);
+    assert.ok(sums.includes('ndjson-line-1') && sums.includes('ndjson-line-2'), `both NDJSON events journaled; got ${sums}`);
+  });
+
+  it('a bare JSON array of events journals every element', async () => {
+    const io = makeIo({
+      files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(mkBundle()) },
+      stdin: JSON.stringify([
+        { type: 'decision', payload: { summary: 'arr-1' } },
+        { type: 'note', payload: { text: 'arr-2' } },
+      ]),
+    });
+    const code = await cmdCheckpoint(['--platform', 'claude-code'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 0);
+    const entries = journalEntries(io);
+    assert.equal(entries.length, 2, 'both array elements journaled');
+  });
+
+  it('NDJSON with a non-event line is still bad-stdin (no partial application)', async () => {
+    const io = makeIo({
+      files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(mkBundle()) },
+      stdin: JSON.stringify({ type: 'decision', payload: { summary: 'good' } }) + '\nnot json at all\n',
+    });
+    const code = await cmdCheckpoint(['--platform', 'claude-code', '--strict'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 1, 'strict mode hard-fails');
+    assert.equal(journalEntries(io).length, 0, 'nothing was journaled from the torn input');
+  });
+
+  it('an array with a non-event element is bad-stdin, not a partial apply', async () => {
+    const io = makeIo({
+      files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(mkBundle()) },
+      stdin: JSON.stringify([{ type: 'decision', payload: { summary: 'good' } }, 'garbage-string']),
+    });
+    const code = await cmdCheckpoint(['--platform', 'claude-code', '--strict'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 1);
+    assert.equal(journalEntries(io).length, 0);
+  });
+});

+describe('checkpoint — schema-miss and bare-event stdin (audit findings 2/13/17)', () => {
+  // A close-miss of the documented wrapper must ERROR, not silently degrade to

    23	/** @type {string[]} */
    24	const dirs = [];
    25	const scratch = (prefix) => {
    26	  const d = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), prefix));
    27	  dirs.push(d);
    28	  return d;
    29	};

    31	  for (const d of dirs) rmSync(d, { recursive: true, force: true });

    34	const spawned = (script, args) =>
    35	  new Promise((resolve) => {
    36	    const child = spawn('node', [script, ...args]);
    37	    let stderr = '';
    38	    let stdout = '';
    39	    child.stdout.on('data', (d) => (stdout += d));
    40	    child.stderr.on('data', (d) => (stderr += d));
    41	    const killer = setTimeout(() => child.kill('SIGKILL'), CHILD_TIMEOUT_MS);
    42	    child.on('close', (code, signal) => {
    43	      clearTimeout(killer);
    44	      resolve({ code, signal, stdout, stderr });
    45	    });
    46	  });
    47	
    48	async function until(pred, ms = 15_000) {
    49	  const t0 = Date.now();
    50	  while (!pred()) {
    51	    if (Date.now() - t0 > ms) throw new Error('barrier timeout');
    52	    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 10));
    53	  }
    54	}

    77	  it('one generation bump, one receive archive, loser rejected with state untouched', async () => {

    83	    // Each child runs its OWN prepare (against the same sealed state) and then
    84	    // its own commit — the faithful competing-receivers race, not a shared
    85	    // single receipt double-committed. Both prepares complete before the
    86	    // barrier; the barrier releases both COMMITs together. The winner bumps the
    87	    // generation/receive_log, so the loser's re-derivation under the lock no
    88	    // longer matches its token → stale-receipt rejection, zero mutation.

    90	import { writeFileSync, existsSync } from 'node:fs';

    94	// and the receivers are only "independent" if their receiving identity

   102	writeFileSync(barrierDir + '/ready-' + idx, '');

   105	writeFileSync(barrierDir + '/done-' + idx, JSON.stringify({ status: r.status }));
   106	process.exit(r.status ?? 1);
   107	`;

     1	// Single Claude Code hook entrypoint. hooks.json routes every event here with
     2	// the event name as argv[0]; this script's only job is to reach the core CLI
     3	// (never re-implement it) and to stay fail-open: a hook must never break the
     4	// host session, so every path returns 0.
     5	
     6	import { spawn } from 'node:child_process';
     7	import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
     8	import * as nodeFs from 'node:fs';
     9	import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
    10	import { checkHandoffTree } from '../../../core/src/util/jail.mjs';
    11	
    12	/** @param {any} io */
    13	const batonBin = (io) => `${io.env?.CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT ?? '.'}/core/bin/baton.mjs`;
    14	
    15	/**
    16	 * execFile-shaped spawn that supports {input}: async execFile has no input
    17	 * option, and a child reading stdin would otherwise block forever (gate-2
    18	 * reviewer-b finding 1). The io contract for this adapter is therefore
    19	 * "promise execFile honoring opts.input as the child's stdin".
    20	 * @param {string} cmd @param {string[]} args @param {{cwd?: string, input?: string, timeout?: number}} [opts]
    21	 * @returns {Promise<{stdout: string, stderr: string}>}
    22	 */
    23	function execFileWithInput(cmd, args = [], opts = {}) {
    24	  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    25	    const child = spawn(cmd, args, { cwd: opts.cwd, timeout: opts.timeout ?? 25_000 });
    26	    let stdout = '';
    27	    let stderr = '';
    28	    child.stdout.on('data', (d) => (stdout += d));
    29	    child.stderr.on('data', (d) => (stderr += d));
    30	    child.on('error', reject);
    31	    child.on('close', (code) => {
    32	      if (code === 0) return resolve({ stdout, stderr });
    33	      const e = Object.assign(new Error(`Command failed: ${cmd}`), { code, stdout, stderr });
    34	      reject(e);
    35	    });
    36	    // Fail-open (audit finding 21): a child that dies without draining a large
    37	    // stdin payload emits an out-of-band EPIPE 'error' on stdin that bypasses
    38	    // every try/catch and crashes the hook with a stack trace. Swallow it —
    39	    // the close handler still reports the child's real exit.
    40	    child.stdin?.on('error', () => {});
    41	    if (typeof opts.input === 'string' && child.stdin) child.stdin.write(opts.input);
    42	    child.stdin?.end();
    43	  });
    44	}
    45	
    46	// Hook-error diagnostics (gate-2 minor 16 / plan §Transcript policy log
    47	// contract): metadata-only entries — never the hook payload — with bounded
    48	// retention (last 50 entries / 7 days). The log rides .handoff/ so the purge
    49	// walk covers it.
    50	const HOOK_LOG_MAX = 50;
    51	const HOOK_LOG_MAX_AGE_MS = 7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
    52	
    53	/** @param {any} io @param {string} event @param {any} err */
    54	function logHookError(io, event, err) {
    55	  try {
    56	    // The diagnostics log rides .handoff/, so it passes the same managed-tree
    57	    // jail as every other write (threat model): refuse to create or write
    58	    // through a symlinked/escaped .handoff. Diagnostics must never be the hole
    59	    // the jail closes elsewhere — and this stays fail-open (skip, don't throw).
    60	    if (!checkHandoffTree(io.cwd, io).ok) return;
    61	    const dir = `${io.cwd}/.handoff/log`;
    62	    io.fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
    63	    const path = `${dir}/hook-errors.jsonl`;
    64	    const nowIso = typeof io.now === 'function' ? io.now() : new Date().toISOString();
    65	    /** @type {any[]} */
    66	    let entries = [];
    67	    try {
    68	      entries = io.fs
    69	        .readFileSync(path, 'utf8')
    70	        .split('\n')
    71	        .filter((/** @type {string} */ l) => l.trim() !== '')
    72	        .map((/** @type {string} */ l) => {
    73	          try {
    74	            return JSON.parse(l);
    75	          } catch {
    76	            return null;
    77	          }
    78	        })
    79	        .filter(Boolean);
    80	    } catch {
    81	      entries = [];
    82	    }
    83	    entries.push({ ts: nowIso, event, code: err?.code ?? null });
    84	    const cutoff = Date.parse(nowIso) - HOOK_LOG_MAX_AGE_MS;
    85	    entries = entries.filter((e) => typeof e.ts === 'string' && Date.parse(e.ts) >= cutoff).slice(-HOOK_LOG_MAX);
    86	    io.fs.writeFileSync(path, entries.map((e) => JSON.stringify(e)).join('\n') + '\n');
    87	  } catch {
    88	    // diagnostics must never break fail-open
    89	  }
    90	}
    91	
    92	/**
    93	 * Run a core baton command as a child process, forwarding the raw hook payload
    94	 * as the child's stdin. Rejections are swallowed (fail-open) — core commands
    95	 * carry their own hook-safety, and Claude ignores hook exit codes anyway —
    96	 * but each failure leaves a metadata-only diagnostic in .handoff/log/.
    97	 * opts.acceptCodes: exit codes that are VERDICTS, not failures (audit finding
    98	 * 20: detect's 10/11/12/13 classification exits were logged as hook errors on
    99	 * every real limit death, drowning the diagnostics log in noise).
   100	 * @param {any} io @param {string[]} argv @param {string} event
   101	 * @param {{timeout?: number, acceptCodes?: number[]}} [opts]
   102	 * @returns {Promise<{stdout: string, stderr: string} | null>}
   103	 */
   104	async function baton(io, argv, event, opts = {}) {
   105	  try {
   106	    return await io.execFile('node', [batonBin(io), ...argv], { input: io.stdin, timeout: opts.timeout });
   107	  } catch (err) {
   108	    if (Array.isArray(opts.acceptCodes) && opts.acceptCodes.includes(/** @type {any} */ (err)?.code)) {
   109	      const e = /** @type {any} */ (err);
   110	      return { stdout: e?.stdout ?? '', stderr: e?.stderr ?? '' };
   111	    }
   112	    // fail-open: a failing child must not break the session
   113	    logHookError(io, event, err);
   114	    return null;
   115	  }
   116	}
   117	
   118	/** @param {any} io @returns {any | null} parsed hook payload, or null */
   119	function parsePayload(io) {
   120	  try {
   121	    return JSON.parse(typeof io.stdin === 'string' ? io.stdin : '');
   122	  } catch {
   123	    return null;
   124	  }
   125	}
   126	
   127	/**
   128	 * SessionStart: not a checkpoint — delegate to the core `session-start`
   129	 * command (audit finding 19: the old inline version read
   130	 * `${io.cwd}/.handoff/bundle.json` raw, so launching Claude Code in a repo
   131	 * SUBDIRECTORY silently never fired the pending-handoff notice while the same
   132	 * session's Stop checkpoints DID land at the discovered toplevel). Core owns
   133	 * root discovery, the managed-tree jail, and the origin allowlist; this shim
   134	 * only forwards the shaped stdout the harness consumes.
   135	 * @param {any} io
   136	 */
   137	async function sessionStart(io) {
   138	  const r = await baton(io, ['session-start', '--platform', 'claude-code'], 'SessionStart', { timeout: 8_000 });
   139	  if (r && typeof r.stdout === 'string' && r.stdout.length > 0) io.stdout.write(r.stdout);
   140	  return 0;
   141	}
   142	
   143	/**
   144	 * @param {string[]} args argv after the script path; args[0] is the hook event
   145	 * @param {any} io injected {cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now, …}
   146	 * @returns {Promise<number>} always 0 — fail-open by construction
   147	 */
   148	export async function runHook(args, io) {
   149	  try {
   150	    const event = args[0] ?? 'unknown';
   151	
   152	    if (event === 'SessionStart') return sessionStart(io);
   153	
   154	    if (event === 'StopFailure') {
   155	      // CHECKPOINT FIRST (audit finding 22): it is the part that must land
   156	      // before the harness's 30s envelope kills the hook — the limit-death
   157	      // checkpoint is the whole point of this event. Explicit per-child
   158	      // timeouts sum under the envelope (18s + 8s < 30s). detect runs second,
   159	      // and its classification exit codes are VERDICTS, not errors (finding
   160	      // 20) — only real failures (1/2/spawn) reach the diagnostics log.
   161	      await baton(io, ['checkpoint', '--platform', 'claude-code', '--trigger', 'StopFailure'], event, { timeout: 18_000 });
   162	      const errorType = parsePayload(io)?.error?.type;
   163	      const detectArgs = ['detect', '--platform', 'claude-code'];
   164	      if (typeof errorType === 'string') detectArgs.push('--structured-error-type', errorType);
   165	      await baton(io, detectArgs, event, { timeout: 8_000, acceptCodes: [0, 10, 11, 12, 13] });
   166	      return 0;
   167	    }
   168	
   169	    // Stop / PreCompact / SessionEnd — and any future event — are routine
   170	    // mechanical checkpoints; core normalize degrades unknown payloads safely.
   171	    await baton(io, ['checkpoint', '--platform', 'claude-code'], event);
   172	    return 0;
   173	  } catch {
   174	    return 0;
   175	  }
   176	}
   177	
   178	// Main entry: hooks.json executes this file directly (`node …/hook.mjs <Event>`),
   179	// so a real invocation must build the real io and run — an export alone is a
   180	// production no-op (gate-2 reviewer-b finding 1).
   181	const isMain = (() => {
   182	  try {
   183	    return process.argv[1] ? import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(process.argv[1]).href : false;
   184	  } catch {
   185	    return false;
   186	  }
   187	})();
   188	
   189	if (isMain) {
   190	  let stdin = '';
   191	  try {
   192	    if (!process.stdin.isTTY) stdin = readFileSync(0, 'utf8');
   193	  } catch {
   194	    stdin = '';
   195	  }
   196	  const io = {
   197	    cwd: process.cwd(),
   198	    env: process.env,
   199	    stdin,
   200	    stdout: process.stdout,
   201	    stderr: process.stderr,
   202	    fs: nodeFs,
   203	    execFile: execFileWithInput,
   204	    now: () => new Date().toISOString(),
   205	  };
   206	  runHook(process.argv.slice(2), io).then(
   207	    (code) => process.exit(code),
   208	    () => process.exit(0),
   209	  );
   210	}

     4	// Per-platform receive pointers and context shapes (plan §adapters; gate-2
     5	// fix 8). Claude Code injects hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext, Cursor
     6	// expects additional_context JSON, Codex surfaces plain hook stdout.
     7	/** @type {Record<string, string>} */
     8	const RESUME_HINT = {
     9	  'claude-code': 'Suggest running /baton:receive to resume that task with remapped roles.',
    10	  codex: "Run 'baton receive --platform codex' or the baton-handoff skill to resume that task with remapped roles.",
    11	  cursor: "Run 'baton receive --platform cursor' to resume that task with remapped roles.",

    14	/**
    15	 * `baton session-start` — the cheap SessionStart gate the adapters call
    16	 * instead of full receive preparation (gate-2 fix 8). Inspects only
    17	 * pending-foreign state: a bundle that is sealed OR limit-hit AND from
    18	 * another platform yields a harness-shaped notice on stdout; every other state
    19	 * — own bundle, open non-limit bundle, no bundle, corrupt tree — is a quiet
    20	 * no-op. Soft paths ALWAYS exit 0 (a session-start notice must never break a
    21	 * session); a mis-parsed invocation is a broken hook manifest, which is loud
    22	 * (exit 2) so init/doctor drift gets noticed. With --json (surface-audit fold)
    23	 * the standard envelope wraps the outcome so machine consumers can tell "no
    24	 * pending handoff" from a swallowed failure: data = {pending, shaped}.
    25	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    26	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    27	 */
    28	export async function cmdSessionStart(args, io) {
    29	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { platform: 'string' });
    30	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'session-start', parsed.error);
    31	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    32	  const finish = (/** @type {boolean} */ pending, /** @type {any} */ shaped) => {
    33	    if (flags.json === true) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { pending, shaped } });
    34	    else if (shaped !== null) io.stdout.write(typeof shaped === 'string' ? `${shaped}\n` : JSON.stringify(shaped) + '\n');
    35	    return 0;
    36	  };
    37	  try {
    38	    const platform = typeof flags.platform === 'string' ? flags.platform : null;
    39	    if (!platform || platformError(platform) !== null) {
    40	      io.stderr.write('baton session-start: --platform <claude-code|codex|cursor> is required — emitting nothing\n');
    41	      return finish(false, null);
    42	    }
    43	
    44	    const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    45	    // Full load (journal replay included): an unsealed limit death may live
    46	    // only as a post-snapshot journal note. Load warnings are deliberately
    47	    // not surfaced — this is a notice path, not a diagnostics path. An UNSAFE
    48	    // managed tree (symlinked .handoff — threat model / F12) is a quiet no-op:
    49	    // never emit context derived from a tree that failed the jail.
    50	    const { bundle, unsafe } = loadBundle(root, io);
    51	    if (bundle === null || unsafe === true) return finish(false, null);
    52	
    53	    const origin = bundle?.origin?.platform;
    54	    const pending = bundle?.handoff?.status === 'sealed' || bundle?.handoff?.reasonClass === 'usage-limit';
    55	    const foreign = typeof origin === 'string' && origin !== platform;
    56	    if (!pending || !foreign) return finish(false, null);
    57	
    58	    const kind = bundle.handoff.status === 'sealed' ? 'sealed' : 'limit-hit before sealing';
    59	    // The bundle is untrusted input (threat model, iter-3 F12): allowlist the
    60	    // origin label — never interpolate a crafted origin string into injected
    61	    // session context.
    62	    const label = typeof origin === 'string' && platformError(origin) === null ? origin : 'another platform';
    63	    const msg = `A handoff bundle from ${label} is pending in .handoff/ (${kind}). ${RESUME_HINT[platform] ?? RESUME_HINT.codex}`;
    64	
    65	    if (platform === 'cursor') return finish(true, { additional_context: msg });
    66	    if (platform === 'claude-code') return finish(true, { hookSpecificOutput: { hookEventName: 'SessionStart', additionalContext: msg } });
    67	    return finish(true, msg);
    68	  } catch {
    69	    return finish(false, null);
    70	  }
    71	}

    10	
    11	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    12	// ADAPTERS wave — RED. Behavioural contract for the Claude Code single hook
    13	// entrypoint, exercised over injected io (mirrors the core command tests:
    14	// cmdX(args, io) -> exit code). The adapter CALLS the core CLI — these tests pin
    15	// the adapter SURFACE only (event→command mapping, the SessionStart context
    16	// shape, fail-open), never core checkpoint/detect logic.
    17	//
    18	// TARGET MODULE: adapters/claude-code/scripts/hook.mjs (sole target).
    19	//
    20	// Source of truth: plan §Claude Code adapter hook table + platform-notes
    21	// §Claude Code (SessionStart injects hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext).
    22	//
    23	// PINNED CONTRACT (open details resolved here):
    24	//   S0. SIGNATURE. `runHook(args, io) => Promise<number>`, where args[0] is the
    25	//       hook event name (passed by hooks.json) and io is the injected object
    26	//       {cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now, …}. The hook payload
    27	//       arrives as the STRING io.stdin (Claude writes hook JSON on stdin).
    28	//   S1. BATON REACH. To run a core command, hook.mjs invokes the core CLI as a
    29	//       child process via io.execFile: it resolves the baton bin as
    30	//       `${io.env.CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/core/bin/baton.mjs` and calls
    31	//       io.execFile(node, [batonBin, <subcmd>, …flags], {input}). Tests assert on
    32	//       the recorded invocation. (Verifier fold V1) EVERY core child call —
    33	//       checkpoint AND detect — must use that resolved bin path and must forward
    34	//       the raw hook payload as the child's stdin: opts.input === io.stdin. An
    35	//       adapter that bypasses the core CLI or drops the payload cannot pass.
    36	//   S2. MAPPING. Stop / PreCompact / SessionEnd -> exactly one `checkpoint
    37	//       --platform claude-code` invocation, identifying the source event.
    38	//       StopFailure -> BOTH a `detect --platform claude-code` invocation (the
    39	//       classify step) AND a `checkpoint --platform claude-code` invocation
    40	//       stamped with a stop-failure trigger; a rate_limit StopFailure carries the
    41	//       rate_limit signal through to the checkpoint (marks the bundle limit-hit).
    42	//   S3. SessionStart is NOT a checkpoint: it spawns NO checkpoint child. Instead
    43	//       it reads <cwd>/.handoff/bundle.json (read-only, via io.fs) and, when a
    44	//       PENDING (sealed OR usage-limit) bundle FROM ANOTHER PLATFORM exists, emits
    45	//       exactly the Claude context shape on stdout:
    46	//         {"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"SessionStart",
    47	//                                  "additionalContext":"…suggest /baton:receive…"}}
    48	//       (Verifier fold V2) The full pending matrix is pinned:
    49	//         positive:  foreign SEALED bundle            -> context
    50	//         positive:  foreign OPEN + reasonClass usage-limit (limit death before
    51	//                    finalize — the primary failure case) -> context
    52	//         negative:  foreign OPEN non-limit bundle    -> no context
    53	//         negative:  own-platform open non-limit      -> no context
    54	//         negative:  no bundle at all                 -> no context
    55	//       proving BOTH disjuncts of "sealed OR limit-hit" AND "foreign only".
    56	//   S4. FAIL-OPEN. Every error path (unparseable stdin, a rejected core
    57	//       invocation, a missing/corrupt bundle) returns 0 and never throws — a hook
    58	//       must never break the host session. Claude ignores the exit code anyway.
    59	//
    60	// "limit-hit" (S2/S3): pinned to the documented handoff fields — a bundle whose
    61	// handoff.status==='sealed' OR handoff.reasonClass==='usage-limit' is "pending".
    62	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    63

    65	const paths = bundlePaths('/repo');

    68	/**
    69	 * Build an io whose execFile RECORDS every invocation. `respond` lets a test
    70	 * control the child's result (resolve/reject); default resolves empty.
    71	 */
    72	function makeHookIo({ stdin = '', files = {}, respond } = {}) {
    73	  const io = makeIo({ env: { CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT: PLUGIN_ROOT }, stdin, files, now: T0 });
    74	  const calls = [];
    75	  io.execFile = (cmd, args = [], opts = {}) => {
    76	    calls.push({ cmd, args: Array.isArray(args) ? args : [], opts: opts || {} });
    77	    if (respond) return respond({ cmd, args, opts });
    78	    return Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
    79	  };
    80	  return { io, calls };
    81	}
    82

    84	const inputOf = (c) => (typeof c.opts.input === 'string' ? c.opts.input : '');

    86	const findSub = (calls, sub) => calls.filter((c) => c.args.includes(sub) || new RegExp(`\\b${sub}\\b`).test(line(c)));

     1	import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';

    11	
    12	const BUILTIN_SIGNATURES = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', '..', 'data', 'signatures.v1.json');
    13	const STALE_AFTER_MS = 12 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
    14	
    15	/** @typedef {{platform: string, origin: string, reason: string, gitSnapshot: any, probes?: any, sessionHint: string, sessionUnstable?: boolean, reasonClass?: string}} ReceiveOpts */
    16	
    17	/**
    18	 * Classify the switch reason, KEEPING confidence (gate-2 iter-2 M4): a
    19	 * low-confidence heuristic (e.g. Cursor's "quota exceeded") must not silently
    20	 * drive an avoid[] decision — the caller warns and defers to explicit intake.
    21	 * @param {string} reason @param {string} originPlatform @param {any} io
    22	 * @returns {{class: string | null, confidence: string | null}}
    23	 */
    24	function classifyReason(reason, originPlatform, io) {
    25	  try {
    26	    const table = loadSignatures({ builtinPath: BUILTIN_SIGNATURES }, io);
    27	    const verdict = classify({ text: reason, exitCode: 0, platform: originPlatform, table, structured: null });
    28	    return verdict.class === 'ok' ? { class: null, confidence: null } : { class: verdict.class, confidence: verdict.confidence };
    29	  } catch {
    30	    return { class: null, confidence: null };
    31	  }
    32	}
    33	
    34	/**
    35	 * Effective intake: the sealed bundle already records who handed off and why,
    36	 * so the 'unknown'/'unspecified' CLI sentinels default from it (audit finding:
    37	 * the documented resume command rendered "Handed off from unknown" straight
    38	 * after sealing a real origin/reason). Explicit intake always wins. Pure and
    39	 * bundle-deterministic, so prepare and commit derive identical tokens.
    40	 * @param {any} bundle @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @returns {ReceiveOpts}
    41	 */
    42	function effectiveIntake(bundle, opts) {
    43	  const originSentinel = opts.origin === 'unknown' || opts.origin === undefined;
    44	  const reasonSentinel = opts.reason === 'unspecified' || opts.reason === undefined;
    45	  const sealedOrigin = typeof bundle?.origin?.platform === 'string' && bundle.origin.platform !== 'unknown' ? bundle.origin.platform : null;
    46	  const sealedReason = typeof bundle?.handoff?.reason === 'string' && bundle.handoff.reason.length > 0 ? bundle.handoff.reason : null;
    47	  return {
    48	    ...opts,
    49	    origin: originSentinel && sealedOrigin ? sealedOrigin : opts.origin,
    50	    reason: reasonSentinel && sealedReason ? sealedReason : opts.reason,
    51	  };
    52	}
    53	
    54	// The bound token inputs, in the fixed order the structured token serializes
    55	// them. The human names power drift diagnosis: a stale-token rejection names
    56	// exactly which input(s) moved instead of listing six guesses.
    57	const TOKEN_FIELDS = /** @type {const} */ ([
    58	  ['generation', 'bundle generation'],
    59	  ['journalSeq', 'journal seq'],
    60	  ['handoffStatus', 'handoff status'],
    61	  ['receives', 'receive count'],
    62	  ['origin', 'intake origin'],
    63	  ['reason', 'intake reason'],
    64	  ['reasonClass', 'intake reason-class'],
    65	  ['configDigest', 'config'],
    66	  ['gitDigest', 'git state'],
    67	  ['probesDigest', 'probe cache'],
    68	  ['platform', 'target platform'],
    69	  ['sessionHint', 'target session'],
    70	]);
    71	const TOKEN_PREFIX = 'rcpt1';
    72	
    73	/**
    74	 * The revision fingerprint the receipt token derives from. Every field is a
    75	 * bound input: any drift between prepare and commit re-derives a different
    76	 * token, so a stale receipt can never commit (plan §Concurrency).
    77	 * @param {string} root @param {any} bundle @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
    78	 * @returns {Record<string, any>}
    79	 */
    80	function tokenInputs(root, bundle, opts, io) {
    81	  let configDigest = 'absent';
    82	  try {
    83	    configDigest = dedupeKey(io.fs.readFileSync(`${root}/baton.config.json`, 'utf8'));
    84	  } catch {
    85	    // no config — 'absent' is itself a bound value
    86	  }
    87	  return {
    88	    generation: bundle.generation,
    89	    journalSeq: bundle.journalSeq,
    90	    // The seal state is a bound input too (gate-2 fix): a competing receiver
    91	    // that lands between this token's derivation and its commit changes
    92	    // status/receive_log, so the loser's token cannot re-derive.
    93	    handoffStatus: bundle.handoff?.status ?? null,
    94	    receives: Array.isArray(bundle.handoff?.receive_log) ? bundle.handoff.receive_log.length : 0,
    95	    origin: opts.origin,
    96	    reason: opts.reason,
    97	    // Explicit --reason-class is a bound intake input (gate-2 iter-2 M4): it
    98	    // changes the avoid[] decision, so a change between prepare and commit is
    99	    // drift that must re-prepare.
   100	    reasonClass: opts.reasonClass ?? null,
   101	    configDigest,
   102	    gitDigest: dedupeKey(opts.gitSnapshot ?? null),
   103	    probesDigest: dedupeKey(opts.probes ?? null),
   104	    platform: opts.platform,
   105	    sessionHint: opts.sessionHint,
   106	  };
   107	}
   108	
   109	/**
   110	 * Structured receipt token: a fixed-order join of per-input digests. Equality
   111	 * still gates commit exactly as the old opaque hash did; the structure only
   112	 * buys DIAGNOSIS — a mismatch can name the drifted input(s).
   113	 * @param {string} root @param {any} bundle @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
   114	 */
   115	function deriveToken(root, bundle, opts, io) {
   116	  const inputs = tokenInputs(root, bundle, opts, io);
   117	  return `${TOKEN_PREFIX}.` + TOKEN_FIELDS.map(([key]) => dedupeKey(inputs[key] ?? null).slice(0, 10)).join('.');
   118	}
   119	
   120	/**
   121	 * Name the inputs whose digests differ between two structured tokens.
   122	 * @param {string} presented @param {string} expected @returns {string[]}
   123	 */
   124	function driftedInputs(presented, expected) {
   125	  const a = presented.split('.');
   126	  const b = expected.split('.');
   127	  if (a[0] !== TOKEN_PREFIX || b[0] !== TOKEN_PREFIX || a.length !== b.length) return [];
   128	  return TOKEN_FIELDS.filter((_, i) => a[i + 1] !== b[i + 1]).map(([, name]) => name);
   129	}
   130	
   131	/**
   132	 * @param {any} bundle @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
   133	 * @returns {string[]} staleness/degradation warnings
   134	 */
   135	function stalenessWarnings(bundle, opts, io) {
   136	  /** @type {string[]} */
   137	  const warnings = [];
   138	  if (bundle.handoff.status === 'open') {
   139	    warnings.push('source bundle is unsealed (open) — the handoff was never finalized; treating this as a degraded (limit-death) receive');
   140	  }
   141	  const ref = bundle.handoff.finalizedAt ?? bundle.updatedAt;
   142	  const ageMs = Date.parse(io.now()) - Date.parse(ref);
   143	  if (Number.isFinite(ageMs) && ageMs > STALE_AFTER_MS) {
   144	    warnings.push(`bundle finalized ${Math.round(ageMs / 3_600_000)}h ago (staleness threshold 12h) — re-verify before continuing`);
   145	  }
   146	  const g = opts.gitSnapshot;
   147	  if (g && bundle.git) {
   148	    if (bundle.git.headSha !== g.headSha) {
   149	      warnings.push(`HEAD moved since capture: bundle recorded ${bundle.git.headSha}, working tree now ${g.headSha}`);
   150	    }
   151	    if (bundle.git.dirty !== g.dirty) {
   152	      warnings.push(`dirty-state mismatch: bundle captured dirty=${bundle.git.dirty}, working tree now dirty=${g.dirty}`);
   153	    }
   154	  }
   155	  return warnings;
   156	}
   157	
   158	/**
   159	 * Phase 1 of the receive transaction: validate, resolve roles, render the
   160	 * resume prompt, and return a one-time receipt token. Mutates NOTHING — the
   161	 * token is self-contained (a fingerprint hash), never a persisted record.
   162	 * @param {string} root @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
   163	 * @returns {{token: string, prompt: string, assignments: Record<string, any>, warnings: string[]}}
   164	 */
   165	export function prepare(root, opts, io) {
   166	  const { bundle, warnings: loadWarnings } = loadBundle(root, io);
   167	  if (bundle === null) throw new Error(`no handoff bundle under ${root}/.handoff — nothing to receive`);
   168	  opts = effectiveIntake(bundle, opts);
   169	
   170	  const warnings = [...loadWarnings, ...stalenessWarnings(bundle, opts, io)];
   171	
   172	  const { config, errors } = loadConfig(root, io);
   173	  /** @type {Record<string, any>} */
   174	  let assignments = {};
   175	  if (config) {
   176	    // The dead origin comes from the BUNDLE first (its sealed reasonClass and
   177	    // recorded origin platform); typed intake only fills the gaps — a user
   178	    // should not need to retype the verbatim limit string to keep roles off
   179	    // the platform that died (gate-2 reviewer-b finding 4). Confidence gates
   180	    // auto-avoidance (gate-2 iter-2 M4): an explicit --reason-class or a sealed
   181	    // reasonClass is trusted; a live text classification only auto-avoids at
   182	    // medium+ confidence, else it warns and defers to explicit intake.
   183	    let reasonClass;
   184	    let confidence;
   185	    if (typeof opts.reasonClass === 'string') {
   186	      reasonClass = opts.reasonClass;
   187	      confidence = 'high'; // explicit user intake
   188	    } else if (typeof bundle.handoff?.reasonClass === 'string') {
   189	      reasonClass = bundle.handoff.reasonClass;
   190	      confidence = 'high'; // trusted seal
   191	    } else {
   192	      const v = classifyReason(opts.reason, opts.origin, io);
   193	      reasonClass = v.class;
   194	      confidence = v.confidence;
   195	    }
   196	    const deadOrigin =
   197	      typeof bundle.origin?.platform === 'string' && bundle.origin.platform !== 'unknown' ? bundle.origin.platform : opts.origin;
   198	    /** @type {string[]} */
   199	    let avoid = [];
   200	    if (reasonClass === 'usage-limit') {
   201	      if (confidence === 'low') {
   202	        warnings.push(
   203	          `the switch reason matched a usage-limit heuristic only at LOW confidence — not auto-avoiding ${deadOrigin}; pass --reason-class usage-limit to confirm the failover away from it`,
   204	        );
   205	      } else {
   206	        avoid = [deadOrigin];
   207	      }
   208	    }
   209	    assignments = resolveRoles({ config, to: opts.platform, avoid, nativeOnly: false, probes: opts.probes ?? null }).assignments;
   210	  } else {
   211	    warnings.push(`role matrix unavailable (${errors.map((e) => e.msg).join('; ')}) — no role table in this prompt`);
   212	  }
   213	
   214	  const prompt = renderResumePrompt({ bundle, assignments, warnings, origin: opts.origin, reason: opts.reason });
   215	  return { token: deriveToken(root, bundle, opts, io), prompt, assignments, warnings };
   216	}
   217	
   218	/**
   219	 * Phase 2: one atomic transition. Re-derives every bound input from current
   220	 * disk state + opts and rejects ANY drift (throws, zero mutation). On match:
   221	 * writes the received seal, archives it via a 'receive' rotation, then opens a
   222	 * fresh writable generation owned by the receiving platform/session.
   223	 * @param {string} root @param {string} token @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io
   224	 * @returns {{adopted: boolean, generation: number, archivedTo: string | null, alreadyCommitted?: boolean}}
   225	 */
   226	export function commit(root, token, opts, io) {
   227	  // ONE operation-scoped lock across revalidation and every mutation (gate-2
   228	  // fix: reviewers a#1/b#11): two competing receivers serialize here, and the
   229	  // loser's re-derivation sees the winner's generation/status/receive_log.
   230	  return withLock(root, io, (fence) => commitLocked(root, token, opts, io, fence));
   231	}
   232	
   233	/**
   234	 * @param {string} root @param {string} token @param {ReceiveOpts} opts @param {any} io @param {string} fence
   235	 * @returns {{adopted: boolean, generation: number, archivedTo: string | null, alreadyCommitted?: boolean}}
   236	 */
   237	function commitLocked(root, token, opts, io, fence) {
   238	  const { bundle } = loadBundle(root, io);
   239	  if (bundle === null) throw new Error(`no handoff bundle under ${root}/.handoff — nothing to receive; run receive --prepare first`);
   240	  opts = effectiveIntake(bundle, opts);
   241	
   242	  const expected = deriveToken(root, bundle, opts, io);
   243	  if (expected !== token) {
   244	    // Idempotency first (audit finding: duplicate-receive corruption): if THIS
   245	    // exact receipt already landed — its digest is recorded on a receive_log
   246	    // entry — the retry is a lost-output re-send, not drift. Report success
   247	    // with zero mutation instead of sending the model into a re-prepare +
   248	    // re-commit loop that executes a second full receive.
   249	    const receipt = dedupeKey(token).slice(0, 16);
   250	    const landed = Array.isArray(bundle.handoff?.receive_log) && bundle.handoff.receive_log.some((/** @type {any} */ e) => e?.receipt === receipt);
   251	    if (landed) {
   252	      return { adopted: false, alreadyCommitted: true, generation: bundle.generation, archivedTo: null };
   253	    }
   254	    const drifted = driftedInputs(token, expected);
   255	    const detail = drifted.length > 0 ? `drifted input(s): ${drifted.join(', ')}` : 'a bound input drifted since prepare';
   256	    throw new Error(
   257	      `receipt token is stale — ${detail}. Re-prepare and retry, passing the SAME intake flags (--origin/--reason/--reason-class) to both prepare and --commit`,
   258	    );
   259	  }
   260	
   261	  const degraded = bundle.handoff.status !== 'sealed';
   262	  const receiveEntry = {
   263	    origin: opts.origin,
   264	    reason: opts.reason,
   265	    at: io.now(),
   266	    // The receipt digest keys the idempotent-retry detection above.
   267	    receipt: dedupeKey(token).slice(0, 16),
   268	    ...(degraded ? { degradedSeal: true } : {}),
   269	  };
   270	
   271	  // The degraded seal must NOT persist a LOW-confidence classification (iter-3
   272	  // F10): a sealed reasonClass is trusted as high-confidence by the next
   273	  // prepare, so laundering a low-confidence heuristic here would silently
   274	  // auto-avoid the origin one hop later. Explicit intake is high-confidence and
   275	  // seals; a live classification seals ONLY at medium+ confidence, else null
   276	  // (the next receive re-classifies the reason text at its true confidence).
   277	  let sealedReasonClass = null;
   278	  if (degraded) {
   279	    if (typeof opts.reasonClass === 'string') {

    27	/**
    28	 * Repo-root discovery (plan §Root discovery): `--root` wins; else the nearest
    29	 * ancestor of io.cwd carrying `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`; else the
    30	 * nearest ancestor carrying `.git` (the repo toplevel — and a boundary the
    31	 * walk never crosses, so a nested repo resolves to itself); else io.cwd.
    32	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
    33	 * @returns {string}
    34	 */
    35	export function resolveRoot(io, flags) {
    36	  if (typeof flags.root === 'string' && flags.root.length > 0) return flags.root;
    37	  // Normalize separators up front (gate-2 iter-2 M2): a Windows cwd arrives
    38	  // backslash-separated, but Node fs accepts forward slashes, so the ascent
    39	  // and the marker checks work in one forward-slash space. The last separator
    40	  // is either kind; the loop stops at a drive/UNC or filesystem root.
    41	  const start = normSep(io.cwd);
    42	  let dir = start;
    43	  while (true) {
    44	    try {
    45	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.handoff')) || io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, 'baton.config.json'))) return dir;
    46	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.git'))) return dir; // repo toplevel; never walk past a .git boundary
    47	    } catch {
    48	      break;
    49	    }
    50	    const cut = dir.lastIndexOf('/');
    51	    if (cut < 0) break;
    52	    const parent = cut === 0 ? '/' : dir.slice(0, cut);
    53	    // At a drive-relative ceiling ("C:") examine the drive ROOT ("C:/") once —
    54	    // a repo can live directly at C:/ (iter-3 F6) — then stop.
    55	    if (/^[A-Za-z]:$/.test(parent)) {
    56	      const driveRoot = `${parent}/`;
    57	      if (driveRoot !== dir) {
    58	        dir = driveRoot;
    59	        continue;
    60	      }
    61	      break;
    62	    }
    63	    if (parent === dir) break;
    64	    dir = parent;
    65	  }
    66	  return start;
    67	}

    85	/**
    86	 * Strict flag parser (surface-audit fold; docs/design/core.md §tooling always
    87	 * specified strict parsing). spec maps each flag name to 'string' | 'boolean';
    88	 * `json` and `root` are implied common flags. Errors — unknown flag, stray
    89	 * positional, missing string-flag value — return {error} so the command exits
    90	 * 2 instead of silently losing intent: the lenient parser let a stray token
    91	 * flip SAFETY flags off (checkpoint/doctor --strict, remap --native-only,
    92	 * receive --print-prompt all read fail-dangerous exit 0).
    93	 * @param {string[]} args
    94	 * @param {Record<string, 'string' | 'boolean'>} spec
    95	 * @returns {{flags: Record<string, string | boolean>, error?: string}}
    96	 */
    97	export function parseFlagsStrict(args, spec) {
    98	  /** @type {Record<string, 'string' | 'boolean'>} */
    99	  const full = { json: 'boolean', root: 'string', ...spec };
   100	  /** @type {Record<string, string | boolean>} */
   101	  const flags = {};
   102	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
   103	    const a = args[i];
   104	    if (!a.startsWith('--')) return { flags, error: `unexpected argument '${a}' — flags are --name [value]` };
   105	    const key = a.slice(2);
   106	    const kind = full[key];
   107	    if (kind === undefined) return { flags, error: `unknown flag --${key}` };
   108	    if (kind === 'boolean') {
   109	      // A following token that is not a flag would have been this flag's
   110	      // silent "value" under the lenient parser; reject it loudly.
   111	      const next = args[i + 1];
   112	      if (next !== undefined && !next.startsWith('--')) {
   113	        return { flags, error: `unexpected token '${next}' after --${key} (a bare flag takes no value)` };
   114	      }
   115	      flags[key] = true;
   116	      continue;
   117	    }
   118	    const next = args[i + 1];
   119	    // A string flag consumes the next token unless it is itself a KNOWN flag
   120	    // (so `--commit --origin x` errors instead of eating '--origin').
   121	    if (next === undefined || (next.startsWith('--') && full[next.slice(2)] !== undefined)) {
   122	      return { flags, error: `--${key} requires a value` };
   123	    }
   124	    flags[key] = next;
   125	    i += 1;
   126	  }
   127	  return { flags };
   128	}
   129	
   130	/**
   131	 * Minimal flag parser: `--key value` pairs (value = next token not starting
   132	 * with --), bare `--key` booleans, positionals collected in order.
   133	 * @param {string[]} args
   134	 * @returns {{flags: Record<string, string | boolean>, positionals: string[]}}
   135	 */
   136	export function parseFlags(args) {
   137	  /** @type {Record<string, string | boolean>} */
   138	  const flags = {};
   139	  /** @type {string[]} */
   140	  const positionals = [];
   141	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
   142	    const a = args[i];
   143	    if (a.startsWith('--')) {
   144	      const key = a.slice(2);
   145	      const next = args[i + 1];
   146	      if (next !== undefined && !next.startsWith('--')) {
   147	        flags[key] = next;
   148	        i += 1;
   149	      } else {
   150	        flags[key] = true;
   151	      }
   152	    } else {
   153	      positionals.push(a);
   154	    }
   155	  }
   156	  return { flags, positionals };
   157	}

    29	 * only under explicit opt-in, must lstat as a regular file, and its content

    34	function captureTranscriptTail(root, raw, io) {

    36	  if (!cfg.ok || cfg.value?.capture?.transcriptTail !== true) return null;

    46	    // untrusted, so its resolved realpath must sit under the repo root, the
    47	    // user's ~/.claude tree (where Claude Code actually writes transcripts),
    48	    // or a configured capture.transcriptDir — never an arbitrary readable file.
    49	    const fileReal = io.fs.realpathSync(path);

    51	    // normalizing '\\'→'/' in the containment check (isContained) could make an
    52	    // out-of-tree sibling like '/repo\\evil/f' appear contained (iter-4 I9).
    53	    // The transcript path is the one fully-untrusted path that reaches
    54	    // containment, so refuse a backslash-bearing realpath off Windows.
